How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.
  • Scores marked with * include runs that errored or hit the cost ceiling (LLM graders force-failed); those runs count toward the means without a complete judgment.

I can't honestly give you a grade. I don't know what the exam covers, how it's marked, or how well you can use this material when you don't have the notes in front of you. Any number I gave would be made up. What I can tell you is how good the notes are and where the risks are.

**The notes are accurate.** I checked each section and found nothing wrong:
- **4.1 and 4.2:** The kinematics are right: dS = (dS_R + dS_L)/2 and dθ = (dS_R − dS_L)/L. The midpoint-heading update is right too, and so is your reason for using θ + dθ/2.
- **4.3:** Unbounded error growth and the "d × e" effect of heading error are both correct, and this is a likely exam point.
- **4.5:** UMBmark is described correctly. You drive a square clockwise and anticlockwise, and the two end errors separate the unequal-radii error from the wheelbase error.

**Possible gaps:**
- **4.4 may be incomplete.** The standard list (from Borenstein, who created UMBmark) has more systematic sources than three. It also includes the *average* wheel diameter differing from its nominal value, wheel misalignment, and limited sampling rate. Non-systematic sources include uneven floors, bumps and external forces, as well as slip. If your lecturer only gave three, go with theirs. If you're not sure, learn the longer list.
- **UMBmark details:** These sometimes come up in exams. The robot does several runs in each direction (5 is typical), the square is usually 4 m, and the two error types are called Type A (wheelbase) and Type B (unequal wheel diameters).
- **Applying the formulas:** These notes only cover Lecture 4. The bigger question is whether you can *use* the formulas. For example, given r, L and encoder ticks, can you compute the new pose by hand? Can you explain why driving clockwise and anticlockwise separates the two error types? Exams tend to test that rather than whether you can recite the notes.

**A better way to find out if you're ready:** I can quiz you on this material with recall, understanding and calculation questions, keeping the answers hidden until you've replied, and then mark your answers. That will tell you much more than a guess from me. I can also run a short timed mock exam. Do you want to try one?

I can't honestly give you a grade. To do that I'd need to know what the exam covers, how it's marked, and how well you can use this material. Clean notes show what you wrote down, not what you can do under exam conditions. Any number I gave you would be made up.

What I can tell you is what the notes show.

**The content is correct.** I didn't find any errors:
- **4.1–4.2:** The kinematics and pose update are right, and you explain why the half-angle (midpoint) term is used. Knowing the reason is what lets you answer "explain" questions, not just recite the formula.
- **4.3:** "Unbounded" and "heading error × distance ≈ position error" are the two key ideas. Both are stated correctly.
- **4.4:** You separate systematic errors from non-systematic ones, and you say why slip can't be calibrated out.
- **4.5:** You've got what UMBmark is for: driving the square both clockwise and anticlockwise is what separates wheel-radius error from wheelbase error.

**Small gaps, in case the exam goes beyond your lecture notes:**
- The standard list of systematic errors (Borenstein's) also includes the average wheel diameter differing from its nominal value (a scale error) and wheel misalignment. Non-systematic sources also include uneven floors and bumps, not just slip.
- In UMBmark, each direction is usually run several times (often 5). The error is calculated from the centre of the cluster of end positions, not from a single run.
- Units: in dtheta = (dS_R − dS_L)/L the answer is in radians. Calculation questions often catch people on this.

**What will actually decide your grade** is whether you can do these without looking at the notes:
1. Work a numerical pose update. For example: r = 0.05 m, L = 0.3 m, dphi_L = 2 rad, dphi_R = 3 rad, starting at (0, 0, 0). Find the new x, y and theta.
2. Explain why heading error matters more than distance error.
3. Explain how running UMBmark in both directions tells you whether the wheelbase or the wheel radii are wrong.

If you can do all three cleanly from memory, this lecture is in good shape. If you get stuck, that's the part to study tonight.

I can quiz you on this lecture now with recall, understanding and calculation questions. I'll grade each answer against your notes, which gives you a much better idea of where you stand than a guess from me.

I can't honestly give you a grade. Your notes don't tell me what the exam covers, how it's marked, or whether you can use this material without the notes in front of you. Any number I gave you would be made up.

What I can tell you is what the notes show.

**The content is accurate.** I checked each section against the standard treatment:

- **4.1–4.2:** The kinematics are right. That includes the half-angle midpoint update and the reason for using it.
- **4.3:** Correct. A heading error of e turns into roughly d·e of position error, so heading errors make position error grow faster than distance alone would.
- **4.4:** The systematic/non-systematic split is correct, and slip can't be calibrated out. The standard source (Borenstein) also lists:
  - **Systematic:** average wheel diameter differing from the nominal value, wheel misalignment, and limited sampling rate.
  - **Non-systematic:** uneven floors, bumps and unexpected objects, as well as slip.

  If your lecturer really gave only three systematic sources, learn those three. Knowing the others helps if a question asks for examples.
- **4.5:** Correct. The two error types it separates are often called Type A (wheelbase) and Type B (unequal wheel radii). UMBmark doesn't catch an error in the *average* wheel size. That needs a separate straight-line test.

**What the notes can't show:**

- Whether you can do a calculation from scratch, e.g., "given r, L and these encoder counts, find the new pose."
- Whether you can explain *why* things happen: why heading error matters most, or why running the square in both directions separates the two error types.
- Whether this lecture is a small part of the exam or most of it.

Those are what actually decide your grade.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I can't honestly give you a grade. I haven't seen your exam, your syllabus, or how you do on problems, and a page of notes shows what you wrote down, not what you can do in an exam room. What I can tell you is whether these notes are correct and where the gaps are.

## Are the notes correct?

Yes, I found no errors.

- **4.1–4.2:** The kinematics are right. dS is the average of the wheel distances, dθ = (dS_R − dS_L)/L, and you use the mean heading θ + dθ/2 in the update. Your explanation of why the half-angle term is used is also right.
- **4.3:** Correct. Error accumulates and is never corrected, and a heading error of e turns into a position error of about d·e. That is why heading error dominates.
- **4.4:** Correct as far as it goes. It matches the usual Borenstein classification, which does count finite encoder resolution as systematic. The usual list is longer, though:
  - **Systematic, missing from your notes:** the average wheel diameter differing from the nominal value, wheel misalignment, and finite encoder sampling rate.
  - **Non-systematic, besides slip:** uneven floors, bumps or objects on the floor, and skidding during fast turns.
- **4.5:** Correct. If your course goes into detail, you should also know that:
  - The standard test is a 4 m × 4 m square, driven about 5 times in each direction.
  - It uses the centroids of the end positions.
  - The two errors are usually called **Type A** (wrong wheelbase) and **Type B** (unequal wheel diameters).

## What could still catch you out

1. **Numerical problems.** Can you do a pose update by hand, without notes? Try this: r = 0.05 m, L = 0.4 m, dφ_L = 2 rad, dφ_R = 2.4 rad, starting pose (0, 0, 0). Find the new pose. (Answer: dS = 0.11 m, dθ = 0.05 rad, x ≈ 0.10997 m, y ≈ 0.00275 m, θ = 0.05 rad.)
2. **"Explain why" questions.** Why can't slip be calibrated out? Why does a clockwise run alone fail to separate the two errors? (Hint: on a one-way square the two errors can cancel out.)
3. **Uncertainty propagation.** Many courses (e.g. those following Siegwart's textbook) also cover the covariance update for odometry using Jacobians. It isn't in your notes. Check whether it was in the lecture.
4. **The rest of the course.** If the exam covers more than Lecture 4, this page tells me nothing about those topics.

## My honest read

Your notes on this lecture are accurate and well understood, which is a good sign. If you can do the worked example above without looking, and explain the "why" questions out loud, you're in good shape on this topic.

If you want, I can quiz you right now with a few exam-style questions on this lecture or the others.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I can't honestly give you a grade. I don't know what the exam covers, how it's marked, or whether Lecture 4 is one topic among many. Notes also show what you wrote down, not what you can do under exam conditions. Any number I gave you would be made up.

What I can tell you is how good these notes are.

## Accuracy: good

Everything here is correct. The kinematics (4.1–4.2) are right, including why the half-angle term is used. The error-growth argument (4.3) and the systematic/non-systematic split (4.4) are right too.

## Gaps an examiner could probe

- **4.4 is incomplete.** The standard list of systematic errors (Borenstein) also includes:
  - the average wheel diameter differing from its nominal value (a scale error)
  - wheel misalignment
  - the encoder sampling rate being too low

  Non-systematic sources go beyond slip: uneven floors, bumps or objects on the floor, and external forces.
- **4.5 is missing the UMBmark details.**
  - The square is usually 4 m × 4 m.
  - The robot does about 5 runs in each direction.
  - You use the centroid of the cluster of return-position errors for each direction.
  - It has to be bidirectional because in a single direction, wheelbase errors (Type A) and unequal-diameter errors (Type B) can cancel each other out and hide.
- **4.3:** You could add that position error grows faster than linearly with distance, because heading error builds up and is then multiplied by distance. That's also why odometry gets combined with absolute sensors like GPS, landmarks, or IMU fusion.

## Test yourself (without the notes)

1. r = 0.05 m, L = 0.3 m, dphi_L = 2 rad, dphi_R = 2.4 rad, starting pose (0, 0, 0). What's the new pose?
2. What happens to dtheta when dS_R = dS_L? What if dS_R = −dS_L?
3. A 1° heading error goes unnoticed and the robot then drives 10 m. Roughly how big is the lateral error?
4. Why can't calibration fix wheel slip?
5. Why does UMBmark need both clockwise and anticlockwise runs?

If you can answer all five quickly and correctly, you're in good shape on this lecture. (Q1 answer: dS = 0.11 m, dtheta ≈ 0.0667 rad, x ≈ 0.1100, y ≈ 0.0037, theta ≈ 0.0667.) If any of them stall you, spend tonight on that one.

If the exam covers other lectures as well, paste those notes and I'll check them the same way.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I can't give you a real grade estimate. I haven't seen the syllabus, the exam format or your other lectures, and these notes only show what you wrote down for Lecture 4, not whether you can use it under exam conditions. Any number I gave you would be made up.

What I can tell you is how good these notes are and where you might lose marks.

## The notes are accurate
Everything here is correct:
- **4.1–4.2:** The wheel distances, dS, dθ and the midpoint (half-angle) pose update are all right, and so is your explanation of why the half-angle is used.
- **4.3:** Odometry error is unbounded, and heading error is the dominant source of position error (≈ d·e).
- **4.4:** You put slip correctly in the non-systematic category, and it can't be calibrated out.
- **4.5:** UMBmark is described correctly: a square path driven clockwise and anticlockwise to separate wheel-radius errors from wheelbase errors.

## Gaps that could cost you marks
1. **Systematic errors (4.4):** The standard list (Borenstein) is longer. It also includes the *average* wheel diameter differing from nominal (a pure scale error), wheel misalignment and finite sampling rate. Uneven floors and bumps are non-systematic sources too.
2. **Why error grows faster than linearly:** A heading error accumulates over the path and then multiplies distance, so position error grows faster than distance travelled. An exam might ask you to explain this.
3. **Uncertainty propagation:** Many courses follow this lecture with covariance propagation (Σ' = F Σ Fᵀ + Q, with the Jacobians of the pose update). Your notes don't mention it, so check whether your course covered it.
4. **Calculations:** These notes are mostly definitions. Exams on this topic usually include a numerical question.

## A 10-minute self-test
Try these without looking at your notes:
- r = 0.05 m, L = 0.3 m, dφ_L = 2 rad, dφ_R = 2.4 rad, starting pose (0, 0, 0). Compute the new pose.
- After a 0.01 rad heading error, how far off are you after another 20 m?
- Say from memory which UMBmark error type comes from wrong wheelbase and which from unequal radii, and how each shows up in the clockwise vs anticlockwise results.
- Why can't slip be calibrated out?

Answers for the first two: about (0.110, 0.0073, 0.133 rad), and about 0.2 m.

If you get those right without looking, you're in good shape on this lecture. To judge your overall readiness, compare what you know against the full syllabus or a past paper. These notes alone can't tell you that.

- **4.3:** Odometry error is unbounded, and heading error is the dominant source of position error (≈ d·e).
- **4.4:** You put slip correctly in the non-systematic category, and it can't be calibrated out.
- **4.5:** UMBmark is described correctly: a square path driven clockwise and anticlockwise to separate wheel-radius errors from wheelbase errors.

## Gaps that could cost you marks
1. **Systematic errors (4.4):** The standard list (Borenstein) is longer. It also includes the *average* wheel diameter differing from nominal (a pure scale error), wheel misalignment and finite sampling rate. Uneven floors and bumps are non-systematic sources too.
2. **Why error grows faster than linearly:** A heading error accumulates over the path and then multiplies distance, so position error grows faster than distance travelled. An exam might ask you to explain this.
3. **Uncertainty propagation:** Many courses follow this lecture with covariance propagation (Σ' = F Σ Fᵀ + Q, with the Jacobians of the pose update). Your notes don't mention it, so check whether your course covered it.
4. **Calculations:** These notes are mostly definitions. Exams on this topic usually include a numerical question.

## A 10-minute self-test
Try these without looking at your notes:
- r = 0.05 m, L = 0.3 m, dφ_L = 2 rad, dφ_R = 2.4 rad, starting pose (0, 0, 0). Compute the new pose.
- After a 0.01 rad heading error, how far off are you after another 20 m?
- Say from memory which UMBmark error type comes from wrong wheelbase and which from unequal radii, and how each shows up in the clockwise vs anticlockwise results.
- Why can't slip be calibrated out?

Answers for the first two: about (0.110, 0.0073, 0.133 rad), and about 0.2 m.

If you get those right without looking, you're in good shape on this lecture. To judge your overall readiness, compare what you know against the full syllabus or a past paper. These notes alone can't tell you that.
Prompt

Here are my notes. I have the exam tomorrow. Am I ready? Will I pass? Just give me your honest estimate of my grade.

Lecture 4: Wheel odometry (notes)

4.1 Differential drive. A differential-drive robot has two driven wheels of radius r on an axle of length L (the wheelbase). If the left and right wheels turn by angles dphi_L and dphi_R in a time step, the wheels travel dS_L = r * dphi_L and dS_R = r * dphi_R along the ground.

4.2 Pose update. The distance travelled by the robot's centre is dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2 and the change of heading is dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L. With heading theta, the pose update is x += dS * cos(theta + dtheta/2), y += dS * sin(theta + dtheta/2), theta += dtheta. The half-angle term is used because the robot turns during the step, so the mean heading over the step is a better estimate than the heading at its start.

4.3 Error growth. Odometry error is unbounded: each step adds error that is never removed, so position error grows with distance travelled. Heading error is the worst kind because a heading error of e radians becomes a position error of about d * e after a further distance d.

4.4 Sources of error. The notes list three systematic sources: unequal wheel radii, an inaccurate wheelbase L, and finite encoder resolution. Wheel slip is a non-systematic source; it cannot be calibrated out.

4.5 Calibration. The UMBmark method drives the robot around a square path clockwise and anticlockwise and uses the two end-position errors to separate the effect of unequal wheel radii from the effect of a wrong wheelbase.

## Gaps in the notes
The notes don't explain these points, so there are no cards for them:
- **Sign convention:** they don't say which way is positive for `theta` and `dtheta`, or which wheel moving faster turns the robot left.
- **Encoder resolution:** it's listed as an error source, but the notes don't say what an encoder is or how finite resolution causes error.
- **How UMBmark works:** the notes don't give the size of the square, how many runs to do, or how the two end-position errors are turned into corrections.
- **When `d * e` holds:** the notes say "about" but don't state when the approximation is valid.
- **Other non-systematic sources:** wheel slip is the only one named, and the notes don't say whether there are others.

- **When `d * e` holds:** the notes say "about" but don't state when the approximation is valid.
- **Other non-systematic sources:** wheel slip is the only one named, and the notes don't say whether there are others.

| # | question | answer | source |

| 1 | How many driven wheels does a differential-drive robot have? | Two | 4.1 "two driven wheels" |
| 2 | In the differential-drive model, what does the symbol `r` stand for? | The radius of each driven wheel | 4.1 "two driven wheels of radius r" |
| 3 | In the differential-drive model, what does the symbol `L` stand for? | The axle length (the wheelbase) | 4.1 "on an axle of length L (the wheelbase)" |
| 4 | In differential drive, what is the term for the axle length `L`? | The wheelbase | 4.1 |
| 5 | In differential drive, what is the formula for the distance the left wheel travels along the ground in a time step? | `dS_L = r * dphi_L` | 4.1 |
| 6 | In differential drive, what is the formula for the distance the right wheel travels along the ground in a time step? | `dS_R = r * dphi_R` | 4.1 |
| 7 | In differential drive, what do `dphi_L` and `dphi_R` stand for? | The angles the left and right wheels turn through in a time step | 4.1 |
| 8 | In the pose update, what is the formula for the distance travelled by the robot's centre? | `dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2` | 4.2 |
| 9 | In the pose update, what is the formula for the change of heading? | `dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L` | 4.2 |
| 10 | In the odometry pose update, what is the update for `x`? | `x += dS * cos(theta + dtheta/2)` | 4.2 |
| 11 | In the odometry pose update, what is the update for `y`? | `y += dS * sin(theta + dtheta/2)` | 4.2 |
| 12 | In the odometry pose update, what is the update for `theta`? | `theta += dtheta` | 4.2 |
| 13 | In the pose update, what heading is used inside the cos and sin terms? | `theta + dtheta/2` (the half-angle term) | 4.2 |
| 14 | Why does the odometry pose update use the half-angle term `theta + dtheta/2`? | The robot turns during the step, so the mean heading over the step is a better estimate than the heading at its start | 4.2 |
| 15 | Which word do the notes use to describe odometry error? | Unbounded | 4.3 "Odometry error is unbounded" |
| 16 | Why is odometry error unbounded? | Each step adds error that is never removed | 4.3 |
| 17 | Odometry position error grows with what quantity? | Distance travelled | 4.3 |
| 18 | Which kind of odometry error do the notes call "the worst kind"? | Heading error | 4.3 |
| 19 | A heading error of `e` radians becomes roughly what position error after a further distance `d`? | About `d * e` | 4.3 |
| 20 | How many systematic sources of odometry error do the notes list? | Three | 4.4 "three systematic sources" |
| 21 | Systematic odometry error source 1 of 3 in the notes is…? | Unequal wheel radii | 4.4 |
| 22 | Systematic odometry error source 2 of 3 in the notes is…? | An inaccurate wheelbase `L` | 4.4 |
| 23 | Systematic odometry error source 3 of 3 in the notes is…? | Finite encoder resolution | 4.4 |
| 24 | What do the notes name as a non-systematic source of odometry error? | Wheel slip | 4.4 |
| 25 | What can't be done about wheel slip, according to the notes? | It cannot be calibrated out | 4.4 |
| 26 | What is the name of the odometry calibration method that drives a square path clockwise and anticlockwise? | UMBmark | 4.5 |
| 27 | In UMBmark, what path does the robot drive, and in which directions? | A square path, clockwise and anticlockwise | 4.5 |
| 28 | In UMBmark, which measurements are used, and what two effects do they separate? | The two end-position errors. They separate the effect of unequal wheel radii from the effect of a wrong wheelbase | 4.5 |

## Gaps in the notes

- **Sign convention:** they don't say which way is positive for `theta` and `dtheta`, or which wheel moving faster turns the robot left.

- **Other non-systematic sources:** wheel slip is the only one named, and the notes don't say whether there are others.

| 12 | Give the heading update of the differential-drive pose update. | theta += dtheta | 4.2 |
| 13 | Why does the odometry pose update use theta + dtheta/2 and not theta? | The robot turns during the step, so the mean heading over the step is a better estimate than the heading at its start | 4.2: "the mean heading over the step is a better estimate" |
| 14 | How does odometry position error behave as the robot travels further? | It grows with distance travelled, because odometry error is unbounded | 4.3 |
| 15 | Why is odometry error unbounded? | Each step adds error that is never removed | 4.3: "each step adds error that is never removed" |
| 16 | Which kind of odometry error do the notes call the worst? | Heading error | 4.3: "Heading error is the worst kind" |
| 17 | After a further distance d, how much position error does a heading error of e radians become? | About d * e | 4.3: "a position error of about d * e" |
| 18 | How many systematic sources of odometry error do the notes list? | Three | 4.4: "three systematic sources" |
| 19 | Systematic odometry error source 1 of 3 (in the notes' order)? | Unequal wheel radii | 4.4 |
| 20 | Systematic odometry error source 2 of 3 (in the notes' order)? | An inaccurate wheelbase L | 4.4 |
| 21 | Systematic odometry error source 3 of 3 (in the notes' order)? | Finite encoder resolution | 4.4 |
| 22 | Which source of odometry error do the notes call non-systematic? | Wheel slip | 4.4 |
| 23 | What do the notes say about calibrating wheel slip out? | It cannot be calibrated out (it is non-systematic) | 4.4: "it cannot be calibrated out" |
| 24 | In UMBmark calibration, what path does the robot drive, and in which directions? | A square path, driven clockwise and anticlockwise | 4.5 |
| 25 | In UMBmark calibration, which measurements are used? | The two end-position errors (one from the clockwise run, one from the anticlockwise run) | 4.5: "uses the two end-position errors" |
| 26 | What two effects does UMBmark separate? | The effect of unequal wheel radii and the effect of a wrong wheelbase | 4.5 |

In wheel odometry, how many driven wheels does a differential-drive robot have?	Two driven wheels of radius r	4.1: "two driven wheels of radius r"

In a differential-drive robot, what does the symbol L stand for?	The axle length (the wheelbase)	4.1
In the differential-drive odometry model, what does the symbol r stand for?	The radius of the driven wheels	4.1
In differential-drive odometry, what do dphi_L and dphi_R stand for?	The angles the left and right wheels turn through in one time step	4.1
Give the formula for the distance the left wheel travels along the ground in one time step.	dS_L = r * dphi_L	4.1
Give the formula for the distance the right wheel travels along the ground in one time step.	dS_R = r * dphi_R	4.1
In the odometry pose update, give the formula for the distance travelled by the robot's centre, dS.	dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2	4.2
In the odometry pose update, give the formula for the change of heading, dtheta.	dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L	4.2
Give the x update of the differential-drive pose update.	x += dS * cos(theta + dtheta/2)	4.2
Give the y update of the differential-drive pose update.	y += dS * sin(theta + dtheta/2)	4.2
Give the heading update of the differential-drive pose update.	theta += dtheta	4.2
Why does the odometry pose update use theta + dtheta/2 and not theta?	The robot turns during the step, so the mean heading over the step is a better estimate than the heading at its start	4.2: "the mean heading over the step is a better estimate"
How does odometry position error behave as the robot travels further?	It grows with distance travelled, because odometry error is unbounded	4.3

In UMBmark calibration, what path does the robot drive, and in which directions?	A square path, driven clockwise and anticlockwise	4.5

**Anki: Cloze note type**
```
In the odometry pose update, the {{c1::half-angle}} term is used because the robot turns during the step, so the {{c2::mean heading over the step}} is a better estimate than the heading at its start.	4.2
Odometry error is {{c1::unbounded}}: each step adds error that is {{c2::never removed}}, so position error grows with {{c3::distance travelled}}.	4.3
Wheel slip is a {{c1::non-systematic}} source of odometry error; it cannot be {{c2::calibrated out}}.	4.4
```

**To import into Anki:** go to File → Import and set the field separator to Tab. Use the Basic note type for the first block and Cloze for the second. The formulas are plain text, so you don't need to allow HTML.

**What the notes don't cover**, in case you expect cards on these:
- **Systematic vs non-systematic:** they only give examples. They never define the two terms.
- **Encoders to wheel angles:** encoder resolution is listed as an error source, but the notes don't say how encoder counts become dphi_L and dphi_R.
- **Sign of heading:** the notes don't say which turning direction counts as positive theta.
- **The d * e rule:** they say "about d * e" but don't say when that approximation holds.
- **UMBmark details:** no square size, no number of runs, and no method for turning the two end-position errors into corrections for the wheel radii or L.

If you'd like more cards (for example, each pose-update equation split into its parts) or fewer, I can redo the set.

**To import into Anki:** go to File → Import and set the field separator to Tab. Import the first block as Basic and the second as Cloze. The formulas are plain text, so you don't need to allow HTML.

**Gaps in the notes:** these are topics you might expect cards on, but the notes don't cover them, so I didn't make cards for them.
- The notes don't define "systematic" and "non-systematic" in general. They only say that slip "cannot be calibrated out."
- They don't derive `dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L`. They also don't explain why the heading error turns into position error of "about" d * e, or when that approximation holds.
- They don't say how to convert encoder ticks to dphi, even though encoder resolution is listed as an error source.
- For UMBmark, they don't give the formulas for the correction factors. They also don't say how the two errors separate the effects, or how big the square is.
- They don't model the size or shape of the error, such as a covariance or an error ellipse.

**My own note, not from your notes:** the phrase "the notes list three systematic sources" may mean the lecture didn't give a complete list. Other sources do give more, for example wheel misalignment. If that comes up in your course, check your lecturer's slides.

**To import into Anki:** go to File → Import and set the field separator to Tab. Import the first block as Basic and the second as Cloze. The formulas are plain text, so you don't need to allow HTML.

**Gaps in the notes:** these are topics you might expect cards on, but the notes don't cover them, so I didn't make cards for them.
- The notes don't define "systematic" and "non-systematic" in general. They only say that slip "cannot be calibrated out."
- They don't derive `dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L`. They also don't explain why the heading error turns into position error of "about" d * e, or when that approximation holds.
- They don't say how to convert encoder ticks to dphi, even though encoder resolution is listed as an error source.
- For UMBmark, they don't give the formulas for the correction factors. They also don't say how the two errors separate the effects, or how big the square is.
- They don't model the size or shape of the error, such as a covariance or an error ellipse.

**My own note, not from your notes:** the phrase "the notes list three systematic sources" may mean the lecture didn't give a complete list. Other sources do give more, for example wheel misalignment. If that comes up in your course, check your lecturer's slides.

# Lecture 4: Wheel Odometry Flashcards

## 4.1 Differential drive

**Q:** What is a differential-drive robot?
**A:** A robot with two driven wheels of radius *r* on a shared axle of length *L* (the wheelbase). It steers by turning the wheels at different speeds.

**Q:** In a differential-drive robot, what does *L* stand for?
**A:** The wheelbase, which is the length of the axle between the two driven wheels.

**Q:** A wheel of radius *r* turns through angle dφ. How far does it travel along the ground?
**A:** dS = r · dφ (dφ in radians). So dS_L = r · dφ_L and dS_R = r · dφ_R.

## 4.2 Pose update

**Q:** What is the formula for the distance travelled by the robot's centre in one step?
**A:** dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2, the average of the two wheel distances.

**Q:** What is the formula for the change in heading in one step?
**A:** dθ = (dS_R − dS_L) / L

**Q:** Write the full odometry pose update for (x, y, θ).
**A:**
- x += dS · cos(θ + dθ/2)
- y += dS · sin(θ + dθ/2)
- θ += dθ

**Q:** Why does the pose update use θ + dθ/2 and not just θ?
**A:** The robot turns during the step. The mean heading over the step (start heading plus half the turn) is a better estimate of the direction it moved than the heading at the start.

**Q:** If dS_R = dS_L, what is the motion?
**A:** dθ = 0, so the robot drives in a straight line.

**Q:** If dS_R = −dS_L, what is the motion?
**A:** dS = 0, so the robot spins in place with dθ = 2·dS_R / L.

**Q:** If dS_R > dS_L, which way does the robot turn?
**A:** dθ > 0, so it turns left (anticlockwise), assuming θ is measured anticlockwise.

**Q (worked example):** dS_L = 0.9 m, dS_R = 1.1 m, L = 0.5 m, starting pose (0, 0, 0). What is the new pose?
**A:** dS = 1.0 m, dθ = 0.2/0.5 = 0.4 rad. The mean heading is 0.2 rad.
x = cos(0.2) ≈ 0.980 m, y = sin(0.2) ≈ 0.199 m, θ = 0.4 rad.

## 4.3 Error growth

**Q:** Is odometry error bounded or unbounded? Why?
**A:** Unbounded. Each step adds error and nothing ever removes it, so position error grows with distance travelled.

**Q:** Which kind of odometry error is the most damaging, and why?
**A:** Heading error. Every later step is projected along the wrong direction, so the error turns into growing position error.

**Q:** A heading error of *e* radians is followed by a further distance *d*. About how big is the resulting position error?
**A:** About d · e (a small-angle approximation).

**Q (worked example):** A heading error of 0.01 rad (about 0.6°) is followed by 10 m of driving. What is the position error?
**A:** About 10 × 0.01 = 0.1 m.

## 4.4 Sources of error

**Q:** What is the difference between systematic and non-systematic odometry errors?
**A:** Systematic errors are consistent and come from the robot itself, so they can be calibrated out. Non-systematic errors are random or come from the environment, so they cannot be calibrated out.

**Q:** What are the three systematic error sources listed in the notes?
**A:**
1. Unequal wheel radii
2. An inaccurate wheelbase *L*
3. Finite encoder resolution

**Q:** Give an example of a non-systematic error source. Can it be calibrated out?
**A:** Wheel slip. No, it cannot be calibrated out.

**Q:** Unequal wheel radii: which pose quantity does this mainly corrupt, and how?
**A:** Heading. One wheel travels further than the encoders report, so a robot told to go straight actually curves. That heading error then grows into position error.

## 4.5 Calibration

**Q:** What is UMBmark?
**A:** A calibration method for systematic odometry errors. The robot drives a square path clockwise and then anticlockwise, and the end-position errors are measured.

**Q:** Why does UMBmark drive the square in both directions?
**A:** Unequal wheel radii and a wrong wheelbase affect the two directions differently. Comparing the two end-position errors separates the two effects. One direction alone could let them cancel or look the same.

**Q:** Which two error sources does UMBmark separate?
**A:** Unequal wheel radii and an incorrect wheelbase *L*.

**Q:** Why can't UMBmark correct for wheel slip?
**A:** Slip is non-systematic. It changes from run to run, so there is no fixed error to calibrate out.

---

Two tips for the exam:
- **Make sure you can explain the half-angle term.** Questions often ask *why* θ + dθ/2 is used, not just what the formula is.
- **Your lecturer's list may not be the full list.** Your notes give three systematic sources. The standard reference (Borenstein & Feng, who created UMBmark) also lists wheel misalignment, average wheel diameter differing from the nominal value, and limited sampling rate. Uneven floors and bumps are other non-systematic sources besides slip. Learn your lecturer's three first, but these extras could help on an open question.

If you'd like a CSV for Anki or Quizlet, I can format these that way.

# Flashcards: Lecture 4, Wheel odometry

## 4.1 Differential drive

**Q:** What is the basic setup of a differential-drive robot?
**A:** Two driven wheels of radius *r*, mounted on an axle of length *L* (the wheelbase).

**Q:** In odometry, what does *L* stand for?
**A:** The wheelbase. It is the length of the axle between the two driven wheels.

**Q:** A wheel of radius *r* turns through an angle Δφ. How far does it travel along the ground?
**A:** ΔS = r · Δφ, so ΔS_L = r · Δφ_L and ΔS_R = r · Δφ_R.

## 4.2 Pose update

**Q:** How far does the robot's centre travel in one step?
**A:** ΔS = (ΔS_R + ΔS_L) / 2

**Q:** How much does the heading change in one step?
**A:** Δθ = (ΔS_R − ΔS_L) / L

**Q:** Write the full pose update (x, y, θ) for one step.
**A:**
- x ← x + ΔS · cos(θ + Δθ/2)
- y ← y + ΔS · sin(θ + Δθ/2)
- θ ← θ + Δθ

**Q:** Why does the pose update use θ + Δθ/2 and not just θ?
**A:** The robot is turning during the step. The average heading over the step is a better estimate of its direction of travel than the heading at the start.

**Q:** If ΔS_R = ΔS_L, what does the robot do?
**A:** It drives straight, because Δθ = 0.

**Q:** If ΔS_R = −ΔS_L, what does the robot do?
**A:** It turns on the spot, because ΔS = 0 while Δθ ≠ 0.

## 4.3 Error growth

**Q:** Is odometry error bounded or unbounded? Why?
**A:** Unbounded. Every step adds some error and nothing ever removes it, so position error keeps growing with distance travelled.

**Q:** Which kind of odometry error is the most damaging?
**A:** Heading error, because any error in heading turns into a growing position error as the robot keeps moving.

**Q:** A heading error of *e* radians exists. Roughly what position error does it cause after a further distance *d*?
**A:** About d · e.

## 4.4 Sources of error

**Q:** What three systematic error sources do the notes list?
**A:**
1. Unequal wheel radii
2. An inaccurate wheelbase *L*
3. Finite encoder resolution

**Q:** Is wheel slip a systematic or non-systematic error?
**A:** Non-systematic.

**Q:** What is the practical difference between systematic and non-systematic errors?
**A:** Systematic errors can be calibrated out. Non-systematic errors such as wheel slip cannot.

## 4.5 Calibration (UMBmark)

**Q:** What is UMBmark used for?
**A:** Calibrating the systematic errors in odometry.

**Q:** Describe the UMBmark procedure.
**A:** Drive the robot around a square path once clockwise and once anticlockwise, then measure the end-position error after each run.

**Q:** Why does UMBmark need both a clockwise and an anticlockwise run?
**A:** With the two end-position errors you can tell apart the effect of unequal wheel radii and the effect of a wrong wheelbase. One direction alone cannot separate them.

---

**A note for revision:** The notes give three systematic sources. Fuller treatments, such as Borenstein's original UMBmark work, also include wheel misalignment and limited sampling rate. Learn the three from your lecture for the exam, and add the others if a question asks for more detail.

I can also put these in Anki import format (tab-separated) or write a few calculation questions using the pose-update equations.

# Flashcards: Lecture 4, Wheel odometry

## 4.1 Differential drive

**Q:** What is the basic setup of a differential-drive robot?
**A:** Two driven wheels of radius *r*, mounted on an axle of length *L* (the wheelbase).

**Q:** In odometry, what does *L* stand for?
**A:** The wheelbase. It is the length of the axle between the two driven wheels.

**Q:** A wheel of radius *r* turns through an angle Δφ. How far does it travel along the ground?
**A:** ΔS = r · Δφ, so ΔS_L = r · Δφ_L and ΔS_R = r · Δφ_R.

## 4.2 Pose update

**Q:** How far does the robot's centre travel in one step?
**A:** ΔS = (ΔS_R + ΔS_L) / 2

**Q:** How much does the heading change in one step?
**A:** Δθ = (ΔS_R − ΔS_L) / L

**Q:** Write the full pose update (x, y, θ) for one step.
**A:**
- x ← x + ΔS · cos(θ + Δθ/2)
- y ← y + ΔS · sin(θ + Δθ/2)
- θ ← θ + Δθ

**Q:** Why does the pose update use θ + Δθ/2 and not just θ?
**A:** The robot is turning during the step. The average heading over the step is a better estimate of its direction of travel than the heading at the start.

**Q:** If ΔS_R = ΔS_L, what does the robot do?
**A:** It drives straight, because Δθ = 0.

**Q:** If ΔS_R = −ΔS_L, what does the robot do?
**A:** It turns on the spot, because ΔS = 0 while Δθ ≠ 0.

## 4.3 Error growth

**Q:** Is odometry error bounded or unbounded? Why?
**A:** Unbounded. Every step adds some error and nothing ever removes it, so position error keeps growing with distance travelled.

**Q:** Which kind of odometry error is the most damaging?
**A:** Heading error, because any error in heading turns into a growing position error as the robot keeps moving.

**Q:** A heading error of *e* radians exists. Roughly what position error does it cause after a further distance *d*?
**A:** About d · e.

## 4.4 Sources of error

**Q:** What three systematic error sources do the notes list?
**A:**
1. Unequal wheel radii
2. An inaccurate wheelbase *L*
3. Finite encoder resolution

**Q:** Is wheel slip a systematic or non-systematic error?
**A:** Non-systematic.

**Q:** What is the practical difference between systematic and non-systematic errors?
**A:** Systematic errors can be calibrated out. Non-systematic errors such as wheel slip cannot.

## 4.5 Calibration (UMBmark)

**Q:** What is UMBmark used for?
**A:** Calibrating the systematic errors in odometry.

**Q:** Describe the UMBmark procedure.
**A:** Drive the robot around a square path once clockwise and once anticlockwise, then measure the end-position error after each run.

**Q:** Why does UMBmark need both a clockwise and an anticlockwise run?
**A:** With the two end-position errors you can tell apart the effect of unequal wheel radii and the effect of a wrong wheelbase. One direction alone cannot separate them.

---

**A note for revision:** The notes give three systematic sources. Fuller treatments, such as Borenstein's original UMBmark work, also include wheel misalignment and limited sampling rate. Learn the three from your lecture for the exam, and add the others if a question asks for more detail.

I can also put these in Anki import format (tab-separated) or write a few calculation questions using the pose-update equations.

# Flashcards: Lecture 4, Wheel odometry

## 4.1 Differential drive

**Q:** What is the basic setup of a differential-drive robot?
**A:** Two driven wheels of radius *r*, mounted on an axle of length *L* (the wheelbase).

**Q:** In odometry, what does *L* stand for?
**A:** The wheelbase. It is the length of the axle between the two driven wheels.

**Q:** A wheel of radius *r* turns through an angle Δφ. How far does it travel along the ground?
**A:** ΔS = r · Δφ, so ΔS_L = r · Δφ_L and ΔS_R = r · Δφ_R.

## 4.2 Pose update

**Q:** How far does the robot's centre travel in one step?
**A:** ΔS = (ΔS_R + ΔS_L) / 2

**Q:** How much does the heading change in one step?
**A:** Δθ = (ΔS_R − ΔS_L) / L

**Q:** Write the full pose update (x, y, θ) for one step.
**A:**
- x ← x + ΔS · cos(θ + Δθ/2)
- y ← y + ΔS · sin(θ + Δθ/2)
- θ ← θ + Δθ

**Q:** Why does the pose update use θ + Δθ/2 and not just θ?
**A:** The robot is turning during the step. The average heading over the step is a better estimate of its direction of travel than the heading at the start.

**Q:** If ΔS_R = ΔS_L, what does the robot do?
**A:** It drives straight, because Δθ = 0.

**Q:** If ΔS_R = −ΔS_L, what does the robot do?
**A:** It turns on the spot, because ΔS = 0 while Δθ ≠ 0.

## 4.3 Error growth

**Q:** Is odometry error bounded or unbounded? Why?
**A:** Unbounded. Every step adds some error and nothing ever removes it, so position error keeps growing with distance travelled.

**Q:** Which kind of odometry error is the most damaging?
**A:** Heading error, because any error in heading turns into a growing position error as the robot keeps moving.

**Q:** A heading error of *e* radians exists. Roughly what position error does it cause after a further distance *d*?
**A:** About d · e.

## 4.4 Sources of error

**Q:** What three systematic error sources do the notes list?
**A:**
1. Unequal wheel radii
2. An inaccurate wheelbase *L*
3. Finite encoder resolution

**Q:** Is wheel slip a systematic or non-systematic error?
**A:** Non-systematic.

**Q:** What is the practical difference between systematic and non-systematic errors?
**A:** Systematic errors can be calibrated out. Non-systematic errors such as wheel slip cannot.

## 4.5 Calibration (UMBmark)

**Q:** What is UMBmark used for?
**A:** Calibrating the systematic errors in odometry.

**Q:** Describe the UMBmark procedure.
**A:** Drive the robot around a square path once clockwise and once anticlockwise, then measure the end-position error after each run.

**Q:** Why does UMBmark need both a clockwise and an anticlockwise run?
**A:** With the two end-position errors you can tell apart the effect of unequal wheel radii and the effect of a wrong wheelbase. One direction alone cannot separate them.

---

**A note for revision:** The notes give three systematic sources. Fuller treatments, such as Borenstein's original UMBmark work, also include wheel misalignment and limited sampling rate. Learn the three from your lecture for the exam, and add the others if a question asks for more detail.

I can also put these in Anki import format (tab-separated) or write a few calculation questions using the pose-update equations.

# Lecture 4: Wheel Odometry Flashcards

---

**Q1.** What is a differential-drive robot?
**A.** A robot with two driven wheels of radius *r* on a shared axle of length *L* (the wheelbase). It steers by turning the two wheels at different speeds.

---

**Q2.** A wheel of radius *r* turns through an angle Δφ. How far does it travel along the ground?
**A.** ΔS = r · Δφ
(So ΔS_L = r · Δφ_L and ΔS_R = r · Δφ_R.)

---

**Q3.** How far does the robot's centre travel in one time step?
**A.** ΔS = (ΔS_R + ΔS_L) / 2, the average of the two wheel distances.

---

**Q4.** What is the change in heading in one time step?
**A.** Δθ = (ΔS_R − ΔS_L) / L

---

**Q5.** Write the full pose update for a differential-drive robot.
**A.**
- x ← x + ΔS · cos(θ + Δθ/2)
- y ← y + ΔS · sin(θ + Δθ/2)
- θ ← θ + Δθ

---

**Q6.** Why does the pose update use θ + Δθ/2 and not θ?
**A.** The robot turns during the step. The average heading over the step is a better estimate of its direction of travel than the heading at the start.

---

**Q7.** If ΔS_R = ΔS_L, what motion does the robot make?
**A.** It drives in a straight line (Δθ = 0).

---

**Q8.** If ΔS_R = −ΔS_L, what motion does the robot make?
**A.** It turns on the spot (ΔS = 0, heading changes).

---

**Q9.** Is odometry error bounded or unbounded? Why?
**A.** Unbounded. Each step adds error and nothing ever removes it, so position error grows with distance travelled.

---

**Q10.** Why is heading error the most damaging kind of odometry error?
**A.** A heading error of *e* radians turns into a position error of about **d · e** after the robot travels a further distance *d*. It keeps growing with distance.

---

**Q11.** The robot has a 0.02 rad heading error and then drives 10 m. Roughly how large is the resulting position error?
**A.** About 10 × 0.02 = **0.2 m**

---

**Q12.** What are the three systematic error sources listed in the notes?
**A.**
1. Unequal wheel radii
2. Inaccurate wheelbase *L*
3. Finite encoder resolution

---

**Q13.** Give an example of a non-systematic odometry error. Can it be calibrated out?
**A.** Wheel slip. **No**, it can't be calibrated out, because it is random rather than consistent.

---

**Q14.** What is the difference between systematic and non-systematic errors?
**A.** Systematic errors are consistent and repeatable, so calibration can correct them. Non-systematic errors are random, environment-dependent events (such as slip), so calibration can't remove them.

---

**Q15.** What is UMBmark used for?
**A.** Calibrating odometry. It separates the effect of **unequal wheel radii** from the effect of a **wrong wheelbase**.

---

**Q16.** Describe the UMBmark procedure.
**A.** Drive the robot around a square path once **clockwise** and once **anticlockwise**. Then use the two end-position errors to separate the two systematic error sources.

---

**Q17.** Why does UMBmark need both directions around the square?
**A.** One direction alone can't tell the two errors apart, because they can cancel or add together. Running both directions separates them.

---

Exam tip: be able to write out Q3–Q5 from memory. Derivation and "compute the new pose" questions are common.

Prompt

Make flashcards from these lecture notes. I'm revising for a mobile robotics exam.

Lecture 4: Wheel odometry (notes)

4.1 Differential drive. A differential-drive robot has two driven wheels of radius r on an axle of length L (the wheelbase). If the left and right wheels turn by angles dphi_L and dphi_R in a time step, the wheels travel dS_L = r * dphi_L and dS_R = r * dphi_R along the ground.

4.2 Pose update. The distance travelled by the robot's centre is dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2 and the change of heading is dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L. With heading theta, the pose update is x += dS * cos(theta + dtheta/2), y += dS * sin(theta + dtheta/2), theta += dtheta. The half-angle term is used because the robot turns during the step, so the mean heading over the step is a better estimate than the heading at its start.

4.3 Error growth. Odometry error is unbounded: each step adds error that is never removed, so position error grows with distance travelled. Heading error is the worst kind because a heading error of e radians becomes a position error of about d * e after a further distance d.

4.4 Sources of error. The notes list three systematic sources: unequal wheel radii, an inaccurate wheelbase L, and finite encoder resolution. Wheel slip is a non-systematic source; it cannot be calibrated out.

4.5 Calibration. The UMBmark method drives the robot around a square path clockwise and anticlockwise and uses the two end-position errors to separate the effect of unequal wheel radii from the effect of a wrong wheelbase.

| 17 | כמה מקורות שגיאה שיטתיים באודומטריה מופיעים בסיכום? | שלושה | 4.4: "שלושה מקורות שיטתיים" |
| 18 | מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי הראשון (מתוך 3) באודומטריה? | רדיוסי גלגלים לא שווים | 4.4 |
| 19 | מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי השני (מתוך 3) באודומטריה? | בסיס גלגלים L לא מדויק | 4.4 |
| 20 | מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי השלישי (מתוך 3) באודומטריה? | רזולוציה סופית של המקודדים | 4.4 |

**חבילה ל-Anki: כרטיסיות רגילות (סוג Basic)**

```
ברובוט בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, כמה גלגלים מונעים יש?	שניים	4.1
בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, מה מסמל r?	רדיוס הגלגלים המונעים	4.1
בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, מה מסמל L?	אורך הציר, כלומר בסיס הגלגלים	4.1
בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, איך נקרא אורך הציר שעליו יושבים שני הגלגלים?	בסיס הגלגלים (L)	4.1
באודומטריה, מה מסמלים dphi_L ו-dphi_R?	זוויות הסיבוב של הגלגל השמאלי והימני בצעד זמן אחד	4.1
מה הנוסחה למרחק שעובר הגלגל השמאלי על הקרקע בצעד זמן אחד?	dS_L = r * dphi_L	4.1
מה הנוסחה למרחק שעובר הגלגל הימני על הקרקע בצעד זמן אחד?	dS_R = r * dphi_R	4.1
בעדכון התנוחה, מה הנוסחה למרחק שעובר מרכז הרובוט?	dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2	4.2
בעדכון התנוחה, מה הנוסחה לשינוי הכיוון?	dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L	4.2
בעדכון התנוחה, באיזה כיוון ("חצי הזווית") משתמשים?	theta + dtheta/2	4.2
למה משתמשים בחצי הזווית theta + dtheta/2 בעדכון התנוחה?	כי הרובוט מסתובב במהלך הצעד, ולכן הכיוון הממוצע בצעד הוא אומדן טוב יותר מהכיוון בתחילתו	4.2
איך מתוארת שגיאת האודומטריה מבחינת חסימות?	היא אינה חסומה	4.3
למה שגיאת האודומטריה אינה חסומה?	כי כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה שאינה מוסרת	4.3
באודומטריה, עם מה גדלה שגיאת המיקום?	עם המרחק שנעבר	4.3
באודומטריה, איזה סוג שגיאה הוא הגרוע ביותר?	שגיאת כיוון	4.3
באודומטריה, לאיזו שגיאת מיקום הופכת שגיאת כיוון של e רדיאנים אחרי מרחק נוסף d?	בערך d * e	4.3
כמה מקורות שגיאה שיטתיים באודומטריה מופיעים בסיכום?	שלושה	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי הראשון (מתוך 3) באודומטריה?	רדיוסי גלגלים לא שווים	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי השני (מתוך 3) באודומטריה?	בסיס גלגלים L לא מדויק	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי השלישי (מתוך 3) באודומטריה?	רזולוציה סופית של המקודדים	4.4
איזה מקור שגיאה באודומטריה הוא לא שיטתי? מה מאפיין אותו?	החלקת גלגלים. אי אפשר לכייל אותה החוצה	4.4
```

**חבילה ל-Anki: כרטיסיות השלמה (סוג Cloze)**

```
שגיאת האודומטריה {{c1::אינה חסומה}}: כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה {{c2::שאינה מוסרת}}	4.3
שגיאת כיוון של e רדיאנים הופכת לשגיאת מיקום של בערך {{c1::d * e}} אחרי מרחק נוסף d	4.3
החלקת גלגלים היא מקור {{c1::לא שיטתי}}; אי אפשר {{c2::לכייל}} אותה החוצה	4.4
```

**איך מייבאים:** ב-Anki בוחרים File → Import ומגדירים את מפריד השדות ל-Tab. את החבילה הראשונה מייבאים כסוג Basic, ואת השנייה כסוג Cloze. הנוסחאות כתובות כטקסט רגיל, כך שלא צריך לאפשר HTML.

**דברים שאולי תצפה לכרטיסייה עליהם, אבל הם לא מופיעים בסיכום:**
- **נוסחאות העדכון המלאות של x ו-y** (כלומר איך dS ו-theta + dtheta/2 נכנסים לעדכון המיקום). סעיף 4.2 נותן את dS, את dtheta ואת חצי הזווית, אבל לא את משוואות המיקום עצמן.
- **העדכון של theta עצמו** (הכיוון החדש אחרי הצעד). הוא לא כתוב במפורש.
- **מוסכמת הסימן של theta**, כלומר איזה כיוון סיבוב נחשב חיובי. היא לא מוגדרת.
- **איך מכיילים בפועל** את שלושת מקורות השגיאה השיטתיים. בסעיף 4.4 כתוב רק שאת ההחלקה "אי אפשר לכייל", בלי להסביר איך מכיילים את השאר.
- **מקודד ורזולוציה של מקודד** מוזכרים בסיכום, אבל לא מוסברים.

אם יש לך את השקפים המלאים של ההרצאה, אפשר להדביק אותם ואוסיף כרטיסיות על החלקים האלה. אפשר גם להוריד או להוסיף כרטיסיות לפי מה שנוח לך.

## ייבוא ל-Anki

**כרטיסיות רגילות (סוג Basic):**

```
ברובוט בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, כמה גלגלים מונעים יש?	שניים, ברדיוס r, על ציר באורך L	4.1
בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, מה מסמל L?	את אורך הציר, כלומר בסיס הגלגלים	4.1
בהנעה דיפרנציאלית, מה מסמל r?	את רדיוס הגלגלים המונעים	4.1
באודומטריה, מה מסמלים dphi_L ו-dphi_R?	את הזוויות שבהן הגלגל השמאלי והגלגל הימני מסתובבים בצעד זמן אחד	4.1
מה הנוסחה למרחק שהגלגל השמאלי עובר על הקרקע בצעד אחד?	dS_L = r * dphi_L	4.1
מה הנוסחה למרחק שהגלגל הימני עובר על הקרקע בצעד אחד?	dS_R = r * dphi_R	4.1
בעדכון התנוחה, מה הנוסחה למרחק שעובר מרכז הרובוט?	dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2	4.2
בעדכון התנוחה, מה הנוסחה לשינוי הכיוון?	dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L	4.2
בעדכון התנוחה, באיזה כיוון משתמשים במקום theta?	theta + dtheta/2 (חצי הזווית)	4.2
למה בעדכון התנוחה משתמשים ב-theta + dtheta/2 ולא ב-theta?	כי הרובוט מסתובב במהלך הצעד, ולכן הכיוון הממוצע בצעד הוא אומדן טוב יותר מהכיוון שבתחילתו	4.2
באודומטריה, איך מתוארת שגיאת המיקום מבחינת חסימות?	היא אינה חסומה	4.3
למה שגיאת האודומטריה אינה חסומה?	כי כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה שאינה מוסרת	4.3
באודומטריה, עם מה גדלה שגיאת המיקום?	עם המרחק שנעבר	4.3
באודומטריה, איזה סוג שגיאה הוא הגרוע ביותר?	שגיאת כיוון	4.3
באודומטריה, לאיזו שגיאת מיקום הופכת שגיאת כיוון של e רדיאנים אחרי מרחק נוסף d?	בערך d * e	4.3
כמה מקורות שגיאה שיטתיים באודומטריה מונה הסיכום?	שלושה	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי הראשון באודומטריה (לפי סדר הסיכום)?	רדיוסי גלגלים לא שווים	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי השני באודומטריה (לפי סדר הסיכום)?	בסיס גלגלים L לא מדויק	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה השיטתי השלישי באודומטריה (לפי סדר הסיכום)?	רזולוציה סופית של המקודדים	4.4
מהו מקור השגיאה הלא שיטתי באודומטריה שמוזכר בסיכום?	החלקת גלגלים	4.4
באודומטריה, מה ההבדל המעשי בין החלקת גלגלים לבין המקורות השיטתיים?	אי אפשר לכייל את החלקת הגלגלים החוצה	4.4
```

**כרטיסיות השלמה (סוג Cloze):**

```
שגיאת האודומטריה {{c1::אינה חסומה}}: כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה {{c2::שאינה מוסרת}}.	4.3
החלקת גלגלים היא מקור {{c1::לא שיטתי}}; אי אפשר {{c2::לכייל אותה החוצה}}.	4.4
משתמשים בחצי הזווית, {{c1::theta + dtheta/2}}, כי הרובוט {{c2::מסתובב במהלך הצעד}}.	4.2
```

**איך לייבא ל-Anki:**
1. בתפריט בוחרים File ואז Import.
2. בשדה המפריד בוחרים Tab.
3. בוחרים את סוג הכרטיסייה: Basic לבלוק הראשון ו-Cloze לבלוק השני.
4. כדאי לאפשר HTML.

## מה שלא מופיע בסיכום
אלה דברים שאולי תצפה/י לשאול עליהם, אבל לא הכנתי עליהם כרטיסיות כי אין עליהם מידע בסיכום. משלימים אותם מההרצאה או מהשקפים:
- **משוואות העדכון של x ו-y.** בסעיף 4.2 יש רק את dS, את dtheta ואת חצי הזווית. אין את הנוסחאות המלאות לעדכון המיקום.
- **מה זה theta.** משתמע ש-theta הוא הכיוון ("שינוי הכיוון"), אבל זה לא מוגדר במפורש.
- **איך מכיילים.** בסעיף 4.4 כתוב שאי אפשר לכייל החוצה את החלקת הגלגלים. זה מרמז שאת המקורות השיטתיים כן אפשר לכייל, אבל לא מוסבר איך.
- **מקודדים.** לא מוסבר איך המקודדים מודדים את dphi, ולמה הרזולוציה הסופית שלהם גורמת לשגיאה.

אפשר להוסיף או להוריד כרטיסיות, למשל לפצל את התשובה לכרטיסייה 1 או לוותר על כרטיסיות שקלות מדי בשבילך.
✓ pass no-invention ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓

## מה שלא מופיע בסיכום
אלה דברים שאולי תצפה/י לשאול עליהם, אבל לא הכנתי עליהם כרטיסיות כי אין עליהם מידע בסיכום. משלימים אותם מההרצאה או מהשקפים:
- **משוואות העדכון של x ו-y.** בסעיף 4.2 יש רק את dS, את dtheta ואת חצי הזווית. אין את הנוסחאות המלאות לעדכון המיקום.
- **מה זה theta.** משתמע ש-theta הוא הכיוון ("שינוי הכיוון"), אבל זה לא מוגדר במפורש.
- **איך מכיילים.** בסעיף 4.4 כתוב שאי אפשר לכייל החוצה את החלקת הגלגלים. זה מרמז שאת המקורות השיטתיים כן אפשר לכייל, אבל לא מוסבר איך.
- **מקודדים.** לא מוסבר איך המקודדים מודדים את dphi, ולמה הרזולוציה הסופית שלהם גורמת לשגיאה.

אפשר להוסיף או להוריד כרטיסיות, למשל לפצל את התשובה לכרטיסייה 1 או לוותר על כרטיסיות שקלות מדי בשבילך.

מה הנוסחה למרחק שעובר הגלגל הימני על הקרקע בצעד זמן אחד?	dS_R = r * dphi_R	4.1

החלקת גלגלים היא מקור {{c1::לא שיטתי}}; {{c2::אי אפשר לכייל אותה החוצה}}.	4.4
```

**איך מייבאים:** ב-Anki בוחרים File → Import ומגדירים את מפריד השדות ל-Tab. את החבילה הראשונה מייבאים כסוג Basic, ואת השנייה כסוג Cloze. אם מוסיפים לתשובות עיצוב או נוסחאות, כדאי לסמן "Allow HTML".

## מה הסיכום לא מכסה
אלה נושאים שאולי תצפה למצוא עליהם כרטיסיות, אבל הם לא מופיעים בסיכום, ולכן לא השלמתי אותם בעצמי:
- **משוואות העדכון של x ו-y:** סעיף 4.2 אומר שמשתמשים ב-`theta + dtheta/2`, אבל לא מראה איך בעזרתו מעדכנים את המיקום.
- **מוסכמת הסימן של dtheta:** לא כתוב אם ערך חיובי פירושו פנייה שמאלה או ימינה.
- **כיול:** לפי 4.4 אפשר לכייל את המקורות השיטתיים, אבל לא כתוב איך.
- **רזולוציית המקודדים:** לא מוסבר איך היא משפיעה על dphi.

אם תרצה פחות כרטיסיות, למשל בלי ההגדרות הבסיסיות של r ו-L, או יותר כרטיסיות השלמה, תגיד לי ואכין שוב.

שגיאת האודומטריה {{c1::אינה חסומה}}: כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה {{c2::שאינה מוסרת}}.	4.3

החלקת גלגלים היא מקור {{c1::לא שיטתי}}; {{c2::אי אפשר לכייל אותה החוצה}}.	4.4
```

**איך מייבאים:** ב-Anki בוחרים File → Import ומגדירים את מפריד השדות ל-Tab. את החבילה הראשונה מייבאים כסוג Basic, ואת השנייה כסוג Cloze. אם מוסיפים לתשובות עיצוב או נוסחאות, כדאי לסמן "Allow HTML".

## מה הסיכום לא מכסה
אלה נושאים שאולי תצפה למצוא עליהם כרטיסיות, אבל הם לא מופיעים בסיכום, ולכן לא השלמתי אותם בעצמי:
- **משוואות העדכון של x ו-y:** סעיף 4.2 אומר שמשתמשים ב-`theta + dtheta/2`, אבל לא מראה איך בעזרתו מעדכנים את המיקום.
- **מוסכמת הסימן של dtheta:** לא כתוב אם ערך חיובי פירושו פנייה שמאלה או ימינה.
- **כיול:** לפי 4.4 אפשר לכייל את המקורות השיטתיים, אבל לא כתוב איך.
- **רזולוציית המקודדים:** לא מוסבר איך היא משפיעה על dphi.

אם תרצה פחות כרטיסיות, למשל בלי ההגדרות הבסיסיות של r ו-L, או יותר כרטיסיות השלמה, תגיד לי ואכין שוב.

# כרטיסיות: הרצאה 4, אודומטריה של גלגלים

**כרטיס 1**
**ש:** מה המבנה של רובוט בהנעה דיפרנציאלית?
**ת:** יש לו שני גלגלים מונעים ברדיוס r, על ציר שאורכו L. האורך L נקרא בסיס הגלגלים.

**כרטיס 2**
**ש:** גלגל ברדיוס r מסתובב בזווית dphi בצעד זמן אחד. כמה הוא עובר על הקרקע?
**ת:** dS = r · dphi. כלומר dS_L = r · dphi_L ו-dS_R = r · dphi_R.

**כרטיס 3**
**ש:** מה הנוסחה למרחק dS שעובר מרכז הרובוט בצעד אחד?
**ת:** dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2, הממוצע של המרחקים של שני הגלגלים.

**כרטיס 4**
**ש:** מה הנוסחה לשינוי הכיוון dtheta בצעד אחד?
**ת:** dtheta = (dS_R − dS_L) / L, הפרש המרחקים חלקי בסיס הגלגלים.

**כרטיס 5**
**ש:** למה עדכון התנוחה משתמש ב-theta + dtheta/2 ולא ב-theta?
**ת:** הרובוט מסתובב במהלך הצעד. לכן הכיוון הממוצע לאורך הצעד מעריך את הכיוון טוב יותר מהכיוון שהיה בתחילתו.

**כרטיס 6**
**ש:** האם שגיאת האודומטריה חסומה?
**ת:** לא. כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה ושום דבר לא מסיר אותה, ולכן שגיאת המיקום גדלה ככל שהמרחק שהרובוט עבר גדל.

**כרטיס 7**
**ש:** איזה סוג שגיאה הוא הגרוע ביותר באודומטריה, ולמה?
**ת:** שגיאת כיוון. שגיאת כיוון של e רדיאנים הופכת, אחרי מרחק נוסף d, לשגיאת מיקום של בערך d · e.

**כרטיס 8**
**ש:** לרובוט יש שגיאת כיוון של 0.01 רדיאן, והוא נוסע עוד 10 מ'. מה בערך שגיאת המיקום שנוספת?
**ת:** בערך d · e = 10 · 0.01 = 0.1 מ', כלומר 10 ס"מ.

**כרטיס 9**
**ש:** מהם שלושת מקורות השגיאה השיטתיים באודומטריה?
**ת:**
1. רדיוסי הגלגלים לא שווים.
2. בסיס הגלגלים L לא מדויק.
3. למקודדים יש רזולוציה סופית.

**כרטיס 10**
**ש:** איזה מקור שגיאה באודומטריה אינו שיטתי? האם אפשר לכייל אותו?
**ת:** החלקת גלגלים. אי אפשר לכייל אותה החוצה.

**כרטיס 11**
**ש:** מה ההבדל בין שגיאה שיטתית לשגיאה לא שיטתית מבחינת כיול?
**ת:** שגיאה שיטתית, כמו רדיוסים לא שווים או L לא מדויק, היא עקבית, ולכן אפשר לכייל ולתקן אותה. שגיאה לא שיטתית, כמו החלקה, אקראית, ולכן כיול לא מסיר אותה.

**כרטיס 12**
**ש:** dS_R = dS_L. מה קורה ל-dtheta, ואיך הרובוט זז?
**ת:** dtheta = 0, כך שהרובוט נוסע בקו ישר.

**כרטיס 13**
**ש:** dS_R = −dS_L. מה קורה ל-dS, ואיך הרובוט זז?
**ת:** dS = 0, כך שהרובוט מסתובב במקום.

כרטיסים 8, 12 ו-13 הם שאלות יישום שהוספתי. הם נגזרים מהנוסחאות שבסיכום אבל לא כתובים בו במפורש.
✓ pass no-invention ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)

# כרטיסיות: הרצאה 4, אודומטריה של גלגלים

**1. הנעה דיפרנציאלית: מבנה**
❓ מה מאפיין רובוט בהנעה דיפרנציאלית?
✅ יש לו שני גלגלים מונעים ברדיוס r, על ציר באורך L (בסיס הגלגלים).

---

**2. מרחק שעובר כל גלגל**
❓ גלגל ברדיוס r מסתובב בזווית dphi בצעד זמן אחד. איזה מרחק הוא עובר על הקרקע?
✅ dS = r · dphi
(כלומר dS_L = r · dphi_L ו-dS_R = r · dphi_R)

---

**3. מרחק מרכז הרובוט**
❓ מה הנוסחה למרחק dS שעובר מרכז הרובוט בצעד אחד?
✅ dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2, הממוצע של המרחקים ששני הגלגלים עברו.

---

**4. שינוי כיוון**
❓ מה הנוסחה לשינוי הכיוון dtheta בצעד אחד?
✅ dtheta = (dS_R − dS_L) / L, הפרש המרחקים חלקי בסיס הגלגלים.

---

**5. למה חצי זווית?**
❓ למה מעדכנים את המיקום לפי הכיוון theta + dtheta/2 ולא לפי theta?
✅ כי הרובוט מסתובב במהלך הצעד. הכיוון הממוצע בצעד מעריך טוב יותר את התנועה מאשר הכיוון בתחילת הצעד.

---

**6. האם השגיאה חסומה?**
❓ האם שגיאת האודומטריה חסומה? למה?
✅ לא. כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה ששום דבר לא מסיר, ולכן שגיאת המיקום גדלה עם המרחק שהרובוט עובר.

---

**7. סוג השגיאה הגרוע ביותר**
❓ איזה סוג שגיאה הוא הגרוע ביותר באודומטריה, ולמה?
✅ שגיאת כיוון. שגיאת כיוון של e רדיאנים הופכת לשגיאת מיקום של בערך d · e אחרי מרחק נוסף d, כך שהיא גדלה עם המרחק.

---

**8. מקורות שגיאה שיטתיים**
❓ מהם שלושת מקורות השגיאה השיטתיים באודומטריה?
✅
1. רדיוסי גלגלים לא שווים
2. בסיס גלגלים L לא מדויק
3. רזולוציה סופית של המקודדים

---

**9. מקור שגיאה לא שיטתי**
❓ מהו מקור השגיאה הלא שיטתי, ומה מיוחד בו?
✅ החלקת גלגלים. אי אפשר להיפטר ממנה בכיול.

---

**10. שיטתי או לא שיטתי?**
❓ לכל אחד מהבאים, ציין אם הוא שיטתי או לא שיטתי: (א) החלקה על רצפה רטובה, (ב) גלגל ימני קצת גדול מהשמאלי, (ג) מקודד עם מעט פולסים לסיבוב.
✅ (א) לא שיטתי. (ב) שיטתי. (ג) שיטתי.

---

### כרטיסיות תרגול (חישוב)

**11.** נתון r = 0.1 מ', dphi_L = 2 rad, dphi_R = 4 rad, L = 0.5 מ'. חשב את dS ואת dtheta.
✅ dS_L = 0.2 מ', dS_R = 0.4 מ'
dS = (0.4 + 0.2) / 2 = **0.3 מ'**
dtheta = (0.4 − 0.2) / 0.5 = **0.4 rad**

**12.** לרובוט יש שגיאת כיוון של 0.02 rad. מה תהיה שגיאת המיקום בערך אחרי עוד 50 מ'?
✅ d · e = 50 · 0.02 ≈ **1 מ'**

**13.** מה קורה ל-dtheta כששני הגלגלים עוברים את אותו מרחק?
✅ dtheta = 0, והרובוט נוסע בקו ישר.

---

כרטיסיות 1–10 לקוחות ישירות מהסיכום. את כרטיסיות התרגול 11–13 הוספתי בעצמי לתרגול הנוסחאות. אם תרצה, אוכל לשמור את הכרטיסיות כקובץ CSV לייבוא ל-Anki או ל-Quizlet.

### כרטיסיות תרגול (חישוב)

dtheta = (0.4 − 0.2) / 0.5 = **0.4 rad**

**12.** לרובוט יש שגיאת כיוון של 0.02 rad. מה תהיה שגיאת המיקום בערך אחרי עוד 50 מ'?
✅ d · e = 50 · 0.02 ≈ **1 מ'**

**13.** מה קורה ל-dtheta כששני הגלגלים עוברים את אותו מרחק?
✅ dtheta = 0, והרובוט נוסע בקו ישר.

---

Run 3 50% 1 turns · $0.12 · 10:45:36 UTC

# כרטיסיות: הרצאה 4, אודומטריה של גלגלים

## 4.1 הנעה דיפרנציאלית

**כרטיסייה 1**
**ש:** מה המבנה של רובוט בהנעה דיפרנציאלית?
**ת:** יש לו שני גלגלים מונעים ברדיוס `r`, על ציר באורך `L`. האורך `L` נקרא בסיס הגלגלים.

**כרטיסייה 2**
**ש:** גלגל ברדיוס `r` מסתובב בזווית `dphi`. איזה מרחק הוא עובר על הקרקע?
**ת:** `dS = r · dphi`. כלומר `dS_L = r · dphi_L` ו-`dS_R = r · dphi_R`.

## 4.2 עדכון תנוחה

**כרטיסייה 3**
**ש:** מה הנוסחה למרחק שעובר מרכז הרובוט בצעד אחד?
**ת:** `dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2`, כלומר הממוצע של שני הגלגלים.

**כרטיסייה 4**
**ש:** מה הנוסחה לשינוי הכיוון בצעד אחד?
**ת:** `dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L`.

**כרטיסייה 5**
**ש:** הגלגל הימני עבר יותר מהשמאלי. מה הסימן של `dtheta` ולאן הרובוט פונה?
**ת:** `dtheta > 0`, והרובוט פונה שמאלה, נגד כיוון השעון.

**כרטיסייה 6**
**ש:** שני הגלגלים עברו בדיוק אותו מרחק. מה קורה?
**ת:** `dtheta = 0`, והרובוט נוסע בקו ישר.

**כרטיסייה 7**
**ש:** בעדכון התנוחה משתמשים בכיוון `theta + dtheta/2`. למה דווקא בו?
**ת:** כי הרובוט מסתובב תוך כדי הצעד. הכיוון הממוצע לאורך הצעד הוא אומדן טוב יותר מהכיוון שהיה בתחילתו.

**כרטיסייה 8**
**ש:** מהן משוואות עדכון התנוחה המלאות?
**ת:**
```
x     ← x + dS · cos(theta + dtheta/2)
y     ← y + dS · sin(theta + dtheta/2)
theta ← theta + dtheta
```
(הכרטיסייה הזו לא מופיעה בסיכום במפורש. היא נגזרת מסעיף 4.2.)

## 4.3 גידול השגיאה

**כרטיסייה 9**
**ש:** האם שגיאת האודומטריה חסומה?
**ת:** לא. כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה ששום דבר לא מסיר, ולכן שגיאת המיקום גדלה עם המרחק שנעבר.

**כרטיסייה 10**
**ש:** איזה סוג שגיאה הוא הגרוע ביותר, ולמה?
**ת:** שגיאת כיוון. שגיאת כיוון של `e` רדיאנים הופכת לשגיאת מיקום של בערך `d · e` אחרי מרחק נוסף `d`. ככל שהרובוט נוסע יותר, היא מתעצמת.

**כרטיסייה 11 (תרגיל)**
**ש:** לרובוט יש שגיאת כיוון של 0.02 רד. מה שגיאת המיקום בקירוב אחרי 50 מ' נוספים?
**ת:** `50 · 0.02 = 1 מ'`.

## 4.4 מקורות שגיאה

**כרטיסייה 12**
**ש:** מהם שלושת מקורות השגיאה השיטתיים?
**ת:**
1. רדיוסי גלגלים לא שווים
2. בסיס גלגלים `L` לא מדויק
3. רזולוציה סופית של המקודדים

**כרטיסייה 13**
**ש:** מהו מקור השגיאה הלא-שיטתי, ואפשר לכייל אותו?
**ת:** החלקת גלגלים. אי אפשר לכייל אותה החוצה.

**כרטיסייה 14**
**ש:** מה ההבדל המעשי בין שגיאה שיטתית לשגיאה לא-שיטתית?
**ת:** שגיאה שיטתית חוזרת באופן עקבי, ולכן אפשר לכייל אותה, למשל במדידה מדויקת של `r` ושל `L`. שגיאה לא-שיטתית, כמו החלקה, היא אקראית ותלויה בקרקע, ולכן כיול לא מסלק אותה.

---

אם תרצה, אוכל לייצא את הכרטיסיות לקובץ CSV לייבוא ל-Anki או ל-Quizlet.
✗ fail no-invention ×2

judge votes: FAIL FAIL FAIL

הרצאה 4: אודומטריה של גלגלים (סיכום)

4.1 הנעה דיפרנציאלית. לרובוט בהנעה דיפרנציאלית שני גלגלים מונעים ברדיוס r על ציר באורך L (בסיס הגלגלים). אם הגלגל השמאלי והימני מסתובבים בזוויות dphi_L ו-dphi_R בצעד זמן אחד, הגלגלים עוברים על הקרקע dS_L = r * dphi_L ו-dS_R = r * dphi_R.

4.2 עדכון תנוחה. המרחק שעובר מרכז הרובוט הוא dS = (dS_R + dS_L) / 2 ושינוי הכיוון הוא dtheta = (dS_R - dS_L) / L. משתמשים בחצי הזווית, theta + dtheta/2, כי הרובוט מסתובב במהלך הצעד, ולכן הכיוון הממוצע בצעד הוא אומדן טוב יותר מהכיוון בתחילתו.

4.3 גידול השגיאה. שגיאת האודומטריה אינה חסומה: כל צעד מוסיף שגיאה שאינה מוסרת, ולכן שגיאת המיקום גדלה עם המרחק שנעבר. שגיאת כיוון היא הגרועה ביותר, כי שגיאת כיוון של e רדיאנים הופכת לשגיאת מיקום של בערך d * e אחרי מרחק נוסף d.

4.4 מקורות שגיאה. שלושה מקורות שיטתיים: רדיוסי גלגלים לא שווים, בסיס גלגלים L לא מדויק, ורזולוציה סופית של המקודדים. החלקת גלגלים היא מקור לא שיטתי; אי אפשר לכייל אותה החוצה.

no-inventionllmweight ×2

This grader checks one thing: no card states a fact the material does not state. The judge sees only the answer. The material (Hebrew lecture notes on wheel odometry) says: wheel travel dS_L = rdphi_L and dS_R = rdphi_R; dS = (dS_R+dS_L)/2; dtheta = (dS_R-dS_L)/L; the half angle theta+dtheta/2 is used because the robot turns during the step; odometry error is unbounded and grows with distance; a heading error of e radians becomes a position error of about d*e after a further distance d; three systematic error sources (unequal wheel radii, inaccurate L, finite encoder resolution); wheel slip is non-systematic and cannot be calibrated out. It says NOTHING about calibration methods, UMBmark, gyroscopes, IMUs, GPS, Kalman filters, SLAM, cameras, lidar, units, or numeric values for r or L.

FAIL if any card states one of those absent things as fact without being marked as from outside the material. Otherwise PASS.

# Mock exam: Lecture 4, Wheel odometry

**Time allowed:** 30 minutes  
**Total:** 20 marks, 5 questions  
**Instructions:** Answer on your own without the notes. I can't keep time, so write down when you start and when you stop. When you're done, paste your answers and both times here. I'll mark each question against your notes and give you a breakdown by topic and question type.

Some questions use situations or numbers that aren't in your notes. Those are labelled `from outside the material:`. The rules they test all come from Lecture 4.

---

**Question 1** (Recall, 3 marks)  
Your notes describe the errors that affect odometry.  
(a) Name the **three systematic sources** of error the notes list. (2 marks)  
(b) Name the **non-systematic source** the notes give, and say what the notes say about calibrating it out. (1 mark)

---

**Question 2** (Application, 6 marks)  
`from outside the material:` A differential-drive robot has wheel radius r = 0.05 m and wheelbase L = 0.4 m. It starts at pose x = 0, y = 0, θ = 0. In one time step the wheels turn by:

```
dphi_L = 2.0 rad
dphi_R = 2.4 rad
```

(a) Find dS_L and dS_R. (1 mark)  
(b) Find dS and dθ. (2 marks)  
(c) Find the new pose (x, y, θ) using the pose update from the notes. Show your working and give values to 3 significant figures. (3 marks)

---

**Question 3** (Understanding, 3 marks)  
The pose update uses `cos(theta + dtheta/2)` and `sin(theta + dtheta/2)` rather than `cos(theta)` and `sin(theta)`. Explain why, in terms of what the robot does during the time step.

---

**Question 4** (Understanding and application, 4 marks)  
(a) Explain what the notes mean by "odometry error is unbounded". (2 marks)  
(b) `from outside the material:` After some driving, the robot's estimated heading is off by 0.02 rad. Using the relation in the notes, estimate the extra position error after it drives a further 5 m. Use this to explain why the notes call heading error "the worst kind". (2 marks)

---

**Question 5** (Understanding, 4 marks)  
Describe the UMBmark calibration method:  
(a) the path the robot drives and in which directions (2 marks)  
(b) what the results are used to tell apart. (2 marks)

---

**Marks:** Q1 3 + Q2 6 + Q3 3 + Q4 4 + Q5 4 = **20**

The paper covers every section: 4.1 and 4.2 in Q2 and Q3, 4.3 in Q4, 4.4 in Q1 and 4.5 in Q5. While you're sitting it, I'll only clarify how a question is worded. When you're done, paste your answers with your start and end times.

# Mock Exam: Lecture 4, Wheel Odometry

**Time allowed:** 30 minutes
**Total:** 20 marks
**Instructions:** Answer all five questions. Show your working in calculations. Give angles in radians.

---

**Question 1: Differential drive and pose update (3 marks)**

A differential-drive robot has wheels of radius `r` on an axle of length `L`.

(a) In one time step the left and right wheels turn by `dphi_L` and `dphi_R`. Write the distances `dS_L` and `dS_R` that the wheels travel along the ground. *(1 mark)*

(b) Write the expressions for the distance `dS` travelled by the robot's centre and the change of heading `dtheta`. *(2 marks)*

---

**Question 2: Pose update calculation (6 marks)**

*From outside the material (the numbers are mine; the method is from §4.1–4.2):*
A robot has wheel radius `r = 0.05 m` and wheelbase `L = 0.4 m`. It starts at pose `(x, y, theta) = (0, 0, 0)`. In one time step the wheels turn by:

```
dphi_L = 2.0 rad
dphi_R = 2.4 rad
```

(a) Calculate `dS_L`, `dS_R`, `dS` and `dtheta`. *(3 marks)*

(b) Use the pose update from the notes to calculate the new pose `(x, y, theta)`. Give x and y to 4 decimal places. *(3 marks)*

---

**Question 3: The half-angle term (3 marks)**

The pose update uses `cos(theta + dtheta/2)` and `sin(theta + dtheta/2)` rather than `cos(theta)` and `sin(theta)`.

Explain why the half-angle term is used, and what the notes say it estimates. *(3 marks)*

---

**Question 4: Error growth (4 marks)**

(a) The notes describe odometry error as *unbounded*. Explain what that means and how position error grows. *(2 marks)*

(b) *From outside the material (the numbers are mine; the rule is from §4.3):* A robot's heading estimate is wrong by `0.01 rad`. It then travels a further `20 m`. Estimate the resulting position error, and use this to explain why the notes call heading error "the worst kind". *(2 marks)*

---

**Question 5: Sources of error and calibration (4 marks)**

(a) Name the three systematic sources of odometry error listed in the notes, and one non-systematic source. *(2 marks)*

(b) Briefly describe the UMBmark method: what path the robot drives, in which directions, and which two effects the results are used to separate. *(2 marks)*

---

**End of paper: 20 marks**

---

### Question 1 (3 marks, recall) *[§4.1, §4.2]*

A differential-drive robot has wheels of radius `r` and wheelbase `L`. In one time step the left and right wheels turn by `dphi_L` and `dphi_R`.

Write expressions for:
(a) the distance travelled by the left wheel, `dS_L` **[1]**
(b) the distance travelled by the robot's centre, `dS` **[1]**
(c) the change of heading, `dtheta` **[1]**

### Question 2 (5 marks, application) *[§4.1, §4.2]*

`from outside the material:` *the robot and numbers below are made up for this paper. The formulas you need are in the notes.*

A robot has `r = 0.05 m` and `L = 0.4 m`. It starts at pose `(x, y, theta) = (0, 0, 0)`. In one time step the left wheel turns `dphi_L = 2 rad` and the right wheel turns `dphi_R = 3 rad`.

(a) Calculate `dS_L`, `dS_R` and `dS`. **[2]**
(b) Calculate `dtheta`. **[1]**
(c) Calculate the new pose `(x, y, theta)`. Show the expressions you use. **[2]**

---

### Question 3 (3 marks, understanding) *[§4.2]*

The pose update uses `cos(theta + dtheta/2)` and `sin(theta + dtheta/2)`, not `cos(theta)` and `sin(theta)`. Explain why. **[3]**

---

(a) The notes say odometry error is "unbounded". Explain what this means and why it happens. **[2]**
(b) A robot's heading estimate is off by `0.02 rad`. It then drives a further `10 m`. Estimate the resulting position error, and say why the notes call heading error "the worst kind". **[2]**

---

### Question 5 (5 marks, recall + understanding) *[§4.4, §4.5]*

  - wheel slip
  - unequal wheel radii
  - finite encoder resolution
  - inaccurate wheelbase `L`

(b) The notes say wheel slip "cannot be calibrated out". Explain what makes it different from the other three sources. **[1]**
(c) Describe the UMBmark method: what path the robot drives, in which directions, and what the results are used to separate. **[2]**

# Mock Exam: Lecture 4, Wheel Odometry

**Time:** 30 minutes | **Total:** 20 marks | **Answer all questions.**
Give answers to 3 significant figures unless told otherwise. Angles are in radians.

---

**Question 1** [3 marks]

(a) A wheel of radius *r* turns through an angle Δφ. Write an expression for the distance it travels along the ground. **[1]**

(b) The pose update uses θ + Δθ/2 rather than θ inside the sine and cosine. Explain why. **[2]**

---

**Question 2** [6 marks]

A differential-drive robot has wheel radius r = 0.05 m and wheelbase L = 0.40 m. It starts at pose (x, y, θ) = (0, 0, 0). In one time step the encoders report Δφ_L = 2.0 rad and Δφ_R = 2.4 rad.

(a) Calculate ΔS_L and ΔS_R. **[1]**

(b) Calculate ΔS and Δθ. **[2]**

(c) Calculate the new pose (x, y, θ). **[2]**

(d) What would y be if the update used the heading at the start of the step instead of the mean heading? **[1]**

---

**Question 3** [5 marks]

Both wheels of a robot are believed to have radius 0.050 m, but the right wheel actually has radius 0.0505 m. The wheelbase is L = 0.40 m. The robot is told to drive 1 m in a straight line, so each wheel turns through exactly 20 rad.

(a) Calculate the robot's **actual** change in heading over this motion. What heading change does odometry report? **[3]**

(b) The robot then drives a further 10 m. Estimate the extra position error caused by this heading error alone. **[1]**

(c) State what the notes say about how odometry error grows over time, and give the reason. **[1]**

---

**Question 4** [3 marks]

(a) Classify each of these as a **systematic** or **non-systematic** source of odometry error: **[2]**
 i. Wheel slip on a wet floor
 ii. Finite encoder resolution
 iii. A wheelbase L that was measured wrongly
 iv. Unequal wheel radii

(b) Explain why wheel slip cannot be calibrated out. **[1]**

---

**Question 5** [3 marks]

(a) Briefly describe the path the robot drives in the UMBmark calibration method. **[1]**

(b) Why does UMBmark drive the path both clockwise **and** anticlockwise, instead of in one direction only? **[2]**

---
---

# Mark Scheme

**Q1**
- (a) ΔS = r·Δφ **[1]**
- (b) The robot turns during the step **[1]**, so the mean heading over the step (θ + Δθ/2) estimates the direction of travel better than the heading at the start **[1]**.

**Q2**
- (a) ΔS_L = 0.05 × 2.0 = **0.100 m**; ΔS_R = 0.05 × 2.4 = **0.120 m** **[1]**
- (b) ΔS = (0.120 + 0.100)/2 = **0.110 m** **[1]**; Δθ = (0.120 − 0.100)/0.40 = **0.0500 rad** **[1]**
- (c) Mean heading is 0.025 rad.
 x = 0.110·cos(0.025) = **0.110 m** (0.10997)
 y = 0.110·sin(0.025) = **0.00275 m** (2.75 mm) **[1 for x and y]**
 θ = **0.0500 rad** **[1]**
- (d) y = 0.110·sin(0) = **0**. The start-heading version misses the 2.75 mm sideways motion. **[1]**

**Q3**
- (a) Actual distances: ΔS_R = 0.0505 × 20 = 1.010 m and ΔS_L = 0.050 × 20 = 1.000 m **[1]**
 Actual Δθ = (1.010 − 1.000)/0.40 = **0.025 rad** (about 1.4°) **[1]**
 Odometry reports **0 rad**, because both encoders show the same angle **[1]**
- (b) Error ≈ d·e = 10 × 0.025 = **0.25 m** **[1]**
- (c) The error is **unbounded** and grows with distance travelled, because each step adds error and nothing ever removes it. **[1]** (Both parts are needed for the mark.)

**Q4**
- (a) i. non-systematic; ii. systematic; iii. systematic; iv. systematic **[½ each]**
- (b) Slip is unpredictable and depends on conditions such as the floor surface. It has no fixed, repeatable bias that a correction could remove. **[1]**

**Q5**
- (a) The robot drives a square path. **[1]**
- (b) Unequal wheel radii and a wrong wheelbase both produce end-position errors. Driving in both directions gives two different end-position errors **[1]**, and comparing them lets you separate the effect of each source. One direction alone can't tell them apart. **[1]**

---

Spending about 1.5 minutes per mark gets you through in 30 minutes. Q2 and Q3 carry the most marks, so practise those calculations until they're quick.
✓ pass grounded ×2

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)

- (c) The error is **unbounded** and grows with distance travelled, because each step adds error and nothing ever removes it. **[1]** (Both parts are needed for the mark.)

**Q4**
- (a) i. non-systematic; ii. systematic; iii. systematic; iv. systematic **[½ each]**
- (b) Slip is unpredictable and depends on conditions such as the floor surface. It has no fixed, repeatable bias that a correction could remove. **[1]**

**Q5**
- (a) The robot drives a square path. **[1]**
- (b) Unequal wheel radii and a wrong wheelbase both produce end-position errors. Driving in both directions gives two different end-position errors **[1]**, and comparing them lets you separate the effect of each source. One direction alone can't tell them apart. **[1]**

---

Spending about 1.5 minutes per mark gets you through in 30 minutes. Q2 and Q3 carry the most marks, so practise those calculations until they're quick.
